Spectro Cloud\",\"url\":\"https://www.spectrocloud.com/government/sovereign-compute\"},{\"title\":\"AI Agent Security & Data Sovereignty for Enterprises | VDF AI\",\"url\":\"https://vdf.ai/resources/ai-agent-security-data-sovereignty/\"},{\"title\":\"From Air-Gapped AI to VPC Deployments: Driving Enterprise AI Security\",\"url\":\"https://squirro.com/squirro-blog/air-gapped-ai-offline-ai\"}]\n\nBased on the web search results, here's what I found about sovereign, air-gapped, self-hosted AI for offensive security, government defense, and on-premise operations:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n**Sovereign AI Architecture for Government & Defense:**\n\nSovereign AI means the entire AI stack — models, training data, embeddings, inference, orchestration, audit logs — runs on infrastructure controlled by the government entity, on national territory, under national law, with no dependency on foreign cloud providers or foreign-controlled model APIs.\n\n**Air-Gapped Deployment Capabilities:**\n\nThe entire AI stack runs inside the customer's environment with no external connectivity, designed for public sector, defense, and organizations with absolute data residency requirements. VDF.AI is designed for fully air-gapped deployment: no outbound network calls, model weights pre-loaded, updates delivered as signed packages over your own channels, and all telemetry routed to your internal SIEM.\n\n**AI Security for Offensive/Defensive Operations:**\n\nGovernments and agencies are deploying Agentic AI for defense, intelligence, and workflow automation to gain geopolitical advantage. F5 AI Guardrails is the primary AI security enforcement layer for government agencies operating in air-gapped and sovereign environments, deployed as a certified Red Hat OpenShift operator within the agency's own Kubernetes environment, operating without external dependencies after initial setup.\n\n**Data Sovereignty & Compliance:**\n\nBy ensuring all data is processed strictly within the customer's own environment, the offerings provide complete data privacy and sovereignty. {"parentUuid":"0de778ac-9089-4fce-ab4b-d369107d3008","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a1ffdb0470e50c82a","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdCTPs4u38muVZVFzJZ2C","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I have gathered comprehensive, cross-verified material across all seven areas. Here is the synthesized report.\n\n---\n\n# State of the Art: Autonomous AI Offensive Security Agents (2025–2026)\n\n**Purpose:** Map exactly what exists, how each system validates a finding (the true differentiator), how it's governed, and where the whitespace is for a fused *oracle-confirmed + cryptographically-provable + hard-governed + sovereign + Claude-powered* agent.\n\n**One-line thesis of the field:** The frontier has bifurcated. On one side, **DARPA AIxCC** proved that the winning pattern is *LLM reasoning wrapped around a deterministic oracle* (fuzzing + symbolic execution + a re-runnable proof-of-vulnerability). On the other, **commercial/open agents** (XBOW, Strix, NodeZero) are racing to productize live-target pentesting — but almost none of them carry the AIxCC-grade proof discipline into live engagements, and *none* of them cryptographically sign their evidence or hard-enforce scope. That gap is the whitespace.\n\nA note on freshness: several sources reference 2026-dated models/rounds (e.g., \"GPT‑5.1‑Codex‑Max,\" \"Claude Mythos,\" \"Gemini 3 Pro,\" RunSybil's Series A). I've attributed the most forward-dated claims to their sources rather than asserting them as settled fact.\n\n---\n\n## 1. XBOW — the closed cloud leader\n\n- **(a) Architecture:** Multi-agent. \"Thousands of short-lived agents, each with a narrow objective, orchestrated by a persistent **Coordinator** and validated by deterministic logic.\" Three roles: **Coordinator** (orchestration), **Sandbox agents** (tactical shell/Python execution inside a shared per-job Docker container, maintaining state across an exploit chain), and a **Validation agent** (end-to-end PoC oracle). The LLM is used as a flexible exploration/hypothesis engine — it \"thinks through problems, sets its own goals, writes custom code, debugs, switches tactics.\" ([xbow.com/blog/top-1-how-xbow-did-it](https://xbow.com/blog/top-1-how-xbow-did-it), [arxiv 2508.20816](https://arxiv.org/html/2508.20816v1))\n- **(b) Validation — the key move:** *Deterministic, non-LLM validators.* XBOW's explicit design principle is to \"use something other than an LLM to verify the vulnerabilities.\" The agent proposes a vuln and emits concrete evidence (a URL/payload); a **non-AI validator** then checks it via **timing checks, canary/flag tokens, and headless-browser execution** — end-to-end PoC execution. \"Report only vulnerabilities that are concretely exploitable.\" This is the closest thing in the commercial world to the AIxCC oracle idea. ([blog.aidanjohn.org](https://blog.aidanjohn.org/2025/09/27/xbow-agentic-pentesting-with-zero.html), [xbow.com/blog/we-ran-1060-autonomous-attacks](https://xbow.com/blog/we-ran-1060-autonomous-attacks))\n- **(c) Governance:** Scope/authorization is **soft** — policy + mandatory human review before submission (required by HackerOne's automated-tools policy). XBOW published an \"ethical considerations / governance framework\" blog, but enforcement is prompt/policy/human-in-loop, not hard. ([xbow.com/blog/ethical-considerations-ai-pentesting](https://xbow.com/blog/ethical-considerations-ai-pentesting))\n- **(d) Provenance:** PoC evidence is reproducible, but **no cryptographic signing / attestation** of findings.\n- **(e) Sovereignty:** Closed-source, cloud SaaS. Not self-hostable.\n- **(f) Numbers:** #1 on HackerOne's US leaderboard (June 2025) in ~90 days; **1,060 vulnerabilities** submitted (54 critical / 242 high / 524 medium / 65 low). On its own **104-challenge benchmark (XBEN)** it matched a 20-year veteran pentester — **28 minutes vs 40 hours (~85×)**. Funding: ~$117M through Series B (Altimeter), then a **$120M Series C at >$1B valuation** (DFJ Growth, Northzone), ~$237M total. ([securityweek.com](https://www.securityweek.com/autonomous-offensive-security-firm-xbow-raises-120m-at-1b-valuation/), [techrepublic.com](https://www.techrepublic.com/article/news-ai-xbow-tops-hackerone-us-leaderboad/))\n- **(g) Limitations:** ~**25% of findings marked \"informative\"/\"not applicable.\"** Strong on technical bugs (SQLi, XSS, RCE), weak on contextual/business-logic and privacy issues. Human oversight is *essential, not optional*. Contributes to the industry-wide **\"AI slop\"** problem — curl reported ~20% of its bug-bounty submissions were AI slop and *ended its bounty program in Jan 2026*; Node.js tightened signal requirements. ([csoonline.com](https://www.csoonline.com/article/4082265/ai-powered-bug-hunting-shakes-up-bounty-industry-for-better-or-worse.html), [techcrunch.com](https://techcrunch.com/2025/07/24/ai-slop-and-fake-reports-are-exhausting-some-security-bug-bounties/))\n\n## 2. Strix (usestrix/strix) — the open-source integration target\n\n- **(a) Architecture:** Open-source multi-agent: **Reconnaissance, Exploitation, Validation, Post-Exploitation** agents that share discoveries in real time and chain vulns. Offensive toolkit: HTTP intercept proxy, browser exploitation, shell/command execution, custom exploit runtime, recon/OSINT, static+dynamic code analysis, vuln knowledge base. Runs in a self-hosted Docker sandbox. ([github.com/usestrix/strix](https://github.com/usestrix/strix), [helpnetsecurity.com](https://www.helpnetsecurity.com/2025/11/17/strix-open-source-ai-agents-penetration-testing/))\n- **(b) Validation — its headline claim:** *\"No PoC, no finding.\"* Strix \"won't report a vulnerability until it has actually exploited it. Every finding ships with a reproducible proof-of-concept … the proof-of-concept requirement is the hallucination filter. If the exploit doesn't fire, the finding doesn't ship.\" This is the same philosophy as XBOW but open. ([hasamba.medium.com](https://hasamba.medium.com/strix-autonomous-ai-agents-for-validated-pentesting-b644f38ae67f), [rohitraj.tech](https://rohitraj.tech/ar/notes/strix-ai-penetration-testing-agent-guide-2026))\n- **(c) Governance:** Prompt/config-level scope only. No hard enforcement layer.\n- **(d) Provenance:** Reproducible PoC bundle, but **no signing/attestation**.\n- **(e) Sovereignty — its strongest card:** **Fully air-gappable.** With local LLMs (Ollama/LMStudio) + the self-hosted Docker sandbox, \"Strix can run fully air-gapped — no data leaves your environment.\" Also supports Anthropic Claude, OpenAI, Gemini. ([protego.me](https://protego.me/blog/strix-ai-pentester-honest-review), [openllm.wavise.com](https://openllm.wavise.com/blog/strix-ai-penetration-testing))\n- **(f) Numbers:** ~36K+ GitHub stars; hit #1 GitHub Trending. On the XBEN 104-challenge benchmark, a tested build solved **100/104 (96%)**, ~19 min/challenge, **~$3.37/challenge** ($337 total). (A separate open-source build is cited at 84.62% on the XBOW benchmark, ahead of MAPTA's ~75% — benchmark builds vary.) ([rohitraj.tech](https://rohitraj.tech/ar/notes/strix-ai-penetration-testing-agent-guide-2026), [medium.com/data-science-collective](https://medium.com/data-science-collective/building-the-leading-open-source-pentesting-agent-architecture-lessons-from-xbow-benchmark-f6874f932ca4))\n- **(g) Limitations:** \"Exploit fired\" is a *weaker* guarantee than a formal re-runnable oracle (it proves *a* PoC ran, not a signed, independently re-verifiable proof); validation quality still rides on LLM capability; no crypto provenance; scope is soft. **Positioning vs others:** it's the credible *open + sovereign* counter to closed XBOW — but it lacks the oracle-grade + crypto + hard-governance stack.\n\n## 3. DARPA AIxCC — the deepest technical frontier (this is the blueprint)\n\n**The competition** (DARPA + ARPA-H, 2023–2025; Final Aug 2025): build a **fully autonomous Cyber Reasoning System (CRS)** — *human interaction strictly prohibited*. The scoring algorithm rewarded (1) **finding** vulnerabilities, (2) **proving** they exist, (3) **correctly patching** them, plus speed and accuracy. Targets were real-world C/C++ and Java OSS with vulnerabilities synthetically injected into commit history. Scale: **~143 hours of autonomous operation, 7 finalists, 53 challenge projects (63 challenges)**, each team given **$85K cloud compute + $50K LLM credits**. Systems collectively found **54 synthetic vulns, patched 43**, and discovered **18 *real* (non-synthetic) 0-days** now being responsibly disclosed. ([darpa.mil/news/2025/aixcc-results](https://www.darpa.mil/news/2025/aixcc-results), [aicyberchallenge.com/finals-winners-announcement](https://aicyberchallenge.com/finals-winners-announcement/), SoK paper [arxiv 2602.07666](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.07666v1))\n\n**Winners:** 1st **Team Atlanta \"Atlantis\"** ($4M — Georgia Tech, Samsung Research, KAIST, POSTECH); 2nd **Trail of Bits \"Buttercup\"** ($3M); 3rd **Theori** ($1.5M).\n\n### Atlantis (1st) — architecture and the \"PoV-as-oracle\" principle\n- **Ensemble philosophy:** *any technique showing unique contribution is incorporated* — it fuses **LLMs + symbolic execution + directed fuzzing + static analysis** for both discovery and repair. ([team-atlanta.github.io](https://team-atlanta.github.io/), [arxiv 2509.14589](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14589))\n- **Structure:** a **CP-MANAGER** builds each challenge project, allocates Azure + LLM budgets, and launches modules: **ATLANTIS-C** (C/C++), **ATLANTIS-Java** (Java), **ATLANTIS-Multilang** (cross-language fuzzing), **ATLANTIS-Patch** (patch generation), and **ATLANTIS-SARIF** (SARIF report assessment). ([zread.ai/Team-Atlanta](https://zread.ai/Team-Atlanta/aixcc-afc-atlantis))\n- **THE validation model (copy this):** *\"The **Proof-of-Vulnerability (PoV) serves as our oracle**. We validate patches by re-running the PoV against patched programs.\"* A vulnerability is only real if a concrete input triggers it; a patch is only accepted if re-running that same PoV no longer triggers it. Deterministic, re-executable, LLM-independent. ([team-atlanta.github.io/blog/post-afc](https://team-atlanta.github.io/blog/post-afc/))\n- **Results:** Atlantis contributed **3 C/C++ + 3 Java real-world bugs** in the finals (of 18 total across all teams) and found a **SQLite3 0-day in the semifinal** autonomously. ([team-atlanta.github.io/blog/post-asc-sqlite](https://team-atlanta.github.io/blog/post-asc-sqlite/))\n\n### Buttercup (2nd, Trail of Bits) — hybrid, cheap, open-source\n- **Multi-agent, no human in loop.** Components: **Orchestration/UI** (+ SigNoz telemetry); **Vulnerability Discovery** via *AI-augmented mutational fuzzing* — it augments **libFuzzer and Jazzer with LLM-generated seed inputs** to reach vulnerable states faster; **Patching** via coordinated agents (root-cause analysis, code-context retrieval, SWE agents); **Program Analysis** (tree-sitter, code-query, call-graph/dependency understanding). ([trailofbits.com/buttercup](https://trailofbits.com/buttercup/), [blog.trailofbits.com](https://blog.trailofbits.com/2025/08/09/trail-of-bits-buttercup-wins-2nd-place-in-aixcc-challenge/))\n- **Validation:** \"**proves each bug and verifies each fix before reporting**\" → held **~90% accuracy.** Notably achieved *using exclusively cheaper, non-reasoning LLMs* — the oracle discipline, not model size, drove reliability.\n- **Now fully open-source** ([blog.trailofbits.com/2025/08/08/buttercup-is-now-open-source](https://blog.trailofbits.com/2025/08/08/buttercup-is-now-open-source/)) — a reference implementation you can study.\n\n**Why AIxCC matters for the design:** it is the only large-scale, adversarially-judged evidence that the *right* architecture is **LLM-as-explorer + deterministic-oracle-as-judge**, spanning discovery→proof→patch→**patch re-verification**. Its limits: the domain was *OSS memory-safety/injected bugs with known fuzz harnesses in a sandbox competition* — **not** live authorized engagements, **not** governed for real-world scope, and **no** cryptographic evidence layer. Generalizing its oracle discipline outside that niche is open ground. ([SoK, arxiv 2602.07666](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07666))\n\n## 4. Google Big Sleep / Naptime — LLM agent finding real 0-days\n\n- **(a) Architecture (Naptime, June 2024):** agent ↔ target codebase, with four tools mirroring a human researcher's hypothesis-driven loop: **Code Browser** (navigate the codebase, Chromium-Code-Search style), **Python interpreter** (sandboxed — generate precise/complex inputs, fuzz), **Debugger** (run the program, observe behavior under inputs), and a **Reporting tool**. Model- and backend-agnostic. Boosted CyberSecEval2 scores up to **20×**. ([projectzero.google/2024/06/project-naptime.html](https://projectzero.google/2024/06/project-naptime.html))\n- **(b) Validation:** the agent **reproduces the crash concretely** in the sandbox (generated input + debugger observation) — a real crash is the proof, not the LLM's assertion.\n- **(c/d/e) Governance/provenance/sovereignty:** internal Google research; closed; no external scope model; no crypto attestation.\n- **(f) Results:** **Big Sleep (Project Zero + DeepMind, Oct 2024)** was the *first public case of an AI agent finding a previously-unknown exploitable memory-safety bug in widely-used real software* — a **SQLite stack buffer underflow**, fixed same day, before release. In 2025 it found multiple real CVEs: **CVE-2025-6965 (SQLite, critical — known only to threat actors; first time an AI foiled in-the-wild exploitation)**, **CVE-2025-9478 (Chrome ANGLE)**, **CVE-2025-9132 (Chrome V8 OOB write)**. ([projectzero.google/2024/10/from-naptime-to-big-sleep.html](https://projectzero.google/2024/10/from-naptime-to-big-sleep.html), [thehackernews.com](https://thehackernews.com/2025/07/google-ai-big-sleep-stops-exploitation.html), [securityonline.info](https://securityonline.info/google-chrome-patches-critical-angle-vulnerability-cve-2025-9478-discovered-by-ai-agent-big-sleep/))\n- **(g) Limitations:** the team calls results **\"highly experimental\"** and noted (as of the SQLite finding) that a **target-specific fuzzer would be at least as effective**; scope is narrow (memory safety in C/C++); best value is root-cause analysis + triage.\n\n## 5. Open-source AI pentest agents\n\n| Tool | Architecture | Validation | Sovereignty | Notes/Limits |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **CAI** (Alias Robotics) | Lightweight extensible framework; agents (web recon, binary exploit) + tools (Nmap, Gobuster, Frida, Hashcat, Burp, Ghidra) + patterns (Red Team Agent, Bug-Bounty Hunter); **300+ model backends** | Largely trusts LLM reasoning + tool output; CAIBench meta-benchmark for eval | **Self-hosted LLMs for air-gapped** (robotics roots) | Claims **156× cost reduction** ($109 vs $17,218) and 3,600× faster vs human testers — vendor-reported. Evolved from PentestGPT authors. ([github.com/aliasrobotics/CAI](https://github.com/aliasrobotics/CAI), [arxiv 2510.24317](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.24317)) |\n| **PentestGPT** | 3 modules — Reasoning / Generation / Parsing, each its own LLM session | Trusts LLM; human-guided | API models (needs GPT-4-class) | Pioneer but **prototype**; authors now recommend CAI. ([github.com/GreyDGL/PentestGPT](https://github.com/GreyDGL/PentestGPT)) |\n| **HackingBuddyGPT** | Minimal (~50 lines): SSH → run shell cmd → feed output back → decide | None (raw LLM loop) | Any backend | Educational/research minimalism. |\n| **PentAGI** (vxcontrol) | Orchestrator + Researcher + Developer + Executor; **3 memory layers** (long-term vector, working, episodic) on PostgreSQL+pgvector; Graphiti/Neo4j knowledge graph; Kali Docker w/ 20+ tools (nmap, Metasploit, sqlmap) | Runs real tools in sandbox; **no human in loop**; trusts agent judgment | Local (Ollama/DeepSeek) + cloud | Broad backend support (OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini/Bedrock/Ollama). ([helpnetsecurity.com](https://www.helpnetsecurity.com/2026/04/22/pentagi-autonomous-ai-penetration-testing/), [github.com/vxcontrol/pentagi](https://github.com/vxcontrol/pentagi)) |\n| **Nebula** | Interactive AI terminal assistant | N/A — **not autonomous**; aids human testing (commands/payloads/analysis) | Local | Not a standalone-finding agent. |\n\n**Common limits:** except Strix's \"must-fire PoC\" gate, this tier mostly **trusts LLM reasoning** for validity → false positives; none sign evidence; scope is prompt-level.\n\n## 6. Commercial autonomous pentest / offensive\n\n- **Horizon3.ai NodeZero:** Autonomous internal/external/cloud/web pentest at scale. **Validation = real, safe exploit execution with proof:** \"for every weakness … see the proofs showing it was exploited by NodeZero, along with downstream impacts\"; pivots and chains weaknesses like an attacker; **1-click verify** re-tests fixes. Scale evidence: in 2024 it exploited **229 CVEs 99,924 times** across customer environments; **50K+ pentests**. Governance: contractual scope + safe-exploitation controls; cloud + downloadable attack host; no crypto attestation. ([horizon3.ai/nodezero](https://horizon3.ai/nodezero/))\n- **Terra Security:** Agentic + **Human-on-the-Loop.** **Ambient agents** do recon, code review, test-gen, reachability, pentest, **exploitability validation**, docs, remediation; **Copilot agents** run *approved, controlled* exploitation. Trained on **business context**; severity based on your business; provides \"proof of exploitability.\" Claims \"when Terra flags a critical vuln, it has **already been verified — no false positives**.\" Human approves intrusive attacks (a governance strength, but human, not hard-technical). Closed/cloud. ([terra.security](https://www.terra.security/), [terra.security/blog/what-human-in-the-loop-means-at-terra-security](https://www.terra.security/blog/what-human-in-the-loop-means-at-terra-security))\n- **RunSybil:** **$40M** (Khosla Ventures, S32; Mar 2026). **Black-box** (no source access); orchestrator agent **\"Sybil\"** drives specialized agents for recon, exploit simulation, vuln chaining; \"mimic hacker intuition.\" Validation details thin publicly. ([runsybil.com/blog](https://www.runsybil.com/blog/runsybil-raises-40m-to-build-the-ai-native-platform-for-offensive-security))\n- **Mindgard:** Red-teaming **OF AI systems** — autonomous, DAST-style continuous red teaming of models/agents (jailbreaks, extraction, evasion); Lancaster University origin. Attacks the model as an adversary would. ([mindgard.ai](https://mindgard.ai/))\n- **OpenAI Aardvark** (adjacent): \"agentic security researcher\" that **autonomously finds, validates, and helps fix** vulns by reasoning over whole codebases; has found **novel CVEs in OSS** and proposes patches. Closed. ([openai.com/index/introducing-aardvark](https://openai.com/index/introducing-aardvark/))\n- **Commercial pattern:** real-exploit-with-proof is now table stakes; **governance = contractual + human-in-loop (soft)**; **all closed/cloud**; **none sign evidence** or hard-enforce scope at the network/syscall layer.\n\n## 7. AI red-teaming *of* AI systems (prompt injection / agent attacks)\n\n- **Microsoft PyRIT** — \"Metasploit for LLMs.\" Multi-turn attack strategies (**Crescendo, TAP, Skeleton Key**), multimodal, broad target support. Scoring subsystem: true/false, Likert, classification, custom scorers — **often powered by LLMs / Azure Content Safety**. ([toxsec.com/p/pyrit-ai-red-teaming](https://www.toxsec.com/p/pyrit-ai-red-teaming))\n- **NVIDIA Garak** — \"Nmap/Nessus for LLMs.\" Broad probe library; detectors mix **string-match + small classifiers + LLM-as-judge**. ([reference.garak.ai](https://reference.garak.ai/en/latest/garak.detectors.judge.html))\n- **Meta CyberSecEval 3** — benchmark suite (8 risks) adding **autonomous offensive cyber ops**, scaling manual ops, automated social engineering. Tested Llama 3 70b/405b as hacking agents: **basic recon OK, failed at exploitation/post-exploitation.** Ships guardrails (CodeShield, LlamaGuard 3). ([arxiv 2408.01605](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01605))\n- **Anthropic** — Frontier Red Team; Cybench in system cards (Claude 3.7 ~⅓ within 5 attempts; **Sonnet 4.5 ~76.5% at 10 attempts**). Sources report an internal \"**Claude Mythos**\" saturated CTF evals (100% pass@1 on 35 Cybench) and was *withheld* due to a too-severe cyber profile (a judgment call, not an RSP requirement) — treat as source-attributed. Cybench declared \"no longer sufficiently informative,\" motivating **CyberGym/CAIBench**. Anthropic also publishes \"Building AI for cyber defenders.\" ([anthropic.com/news/strategic-warning...](https://www.anthropic.com/news/strategic-warning-for-ai-risk-progress-and-insights-from-our-frontier-red-team), [cybench.github.io](https://cybench.github.io/))\n- **The critical weakness here (feeds the whitespace):** **LLM-as-judge validation is unreliable.** \"One Token to Fool LLM-as-a-Judge\" shows punctuation-only responses (`\":\"`) induce up to **35% false positives in GPT-4o**, and `\"Thought process:\"` drives **60–90% FP** in some open models. Both Garak and PyRIT \"commonly … require manual inspection\" of results due to false positives. Any system that *trusts an LLM to judge success* inherits this. ([arxiv 2507.08794](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.08794), [databricks.com blog](https://www.databricks.com/blog/ai-security-action-applying-nvidias-garak-llms-databricks))\n\n---\n\n## The validation-differentiator matrix (the single most important axis)\n\n| System | How it decides a finding is real | Trust model |\n|---|---|---|\n| **AIxCC / Atlantis / Buttercup** | **Re-runnable Proof-of-Vulnerability oracle**; patch verified by re-running PoV | Deterministic — *gold standard* (but OSS memory-bugs, sandbox only) |\n| **Big Sleep** | Concrete crash reproduced in sandbox + debugger | Deterministic, narrow (memory safety) |\n| **XBOW** | Non-LLM validators: timing/canary/headless-browser, end-to-end PoC | Deterministic-ish, heuristic, **closed** |\n| **Strix** | \"Exploit must fire\" → reproducible PoC or no finding | PoC-gate (weaker than a formal oracle), **open** |\n| **NodeZero / Terra** | Real safe exploit + proof (+ human approval, Terra) | Deterministic exploit, but **closed, human-gated, no crypto** |\n| **CAI / PentAGI / PentestGPT / HackingBuddy** | Mostly LLM reasoning + tool output | **Trusts the LLM** (FP-prone) |\n| **PyRIT / Garak / CyberSecEval** | String-match / classifier / **LLM-as-judge** | LLM-as-judge = **35–90% FP failure modes** |\n\n**Governance reality across the board:** every system enforces scope at the **prompt / policy / human-review** level. The **OWASP Autonomous Penetration Testing Standard (APTS)** now codifies requirements (scope enforcement, safe autonomy, manipulation resistance, accountability, second-precision temporal bounds) — but it's an aspirational *standard*, not something any product hard-enforces at the network/syscall layer today. ([owasp.org/APTS](https://owasp.org/APTS/standard/1_Scope_Enforcement/))\n\n**Provenance reality:** *no* offensive agent surveyed cryptographically signs its findings. The building blocks exist elsewhere — proof-of-exploit reporting, TEE/Nitro-enclave attestation, in-toto/SLSA provenance, even zero-knowledge compilation provenance — but nobody has fused them onto autonomous offensive findings. ([aws attestation blog](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/web3/verify-enclave-counterparties-with-reproducible-builds-and-cryptographic-attestation-using-aws-nitro-enclaves/), [arxiv 2602.11887](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.11887))\n\n**Sovereignty reality:** the *leaders* (XBOW, NodeZero, Terra, RunSybil, Big Sleep, Aardvark) are **cloud/closed**. The *sovereign-capable* tier (Strix, CAI, PentAGI) lacks the oracle + crypto + hard-governance stack. No one occupies both.\n\n---\n\n## The competitive whitespace — what a fused agent could own\n\nNo existing system holds more than **two** of the following five properties at once. A tool that fuses **all five** is genuinely 1-of-1:\n\n1. **Oracle-confirmed validation, generalized beyond the AIxCC niche.** AIxCC proved *PoV-as-oracle* (deterministic re-execution proves the bug *and* verifies the fix), but only for OSS memory-safety bugs in a sandbox. XBOW's validators and Strix's \"must-fire PoC\" gesture at this for live targets but are heuristic/weaker. **Whitespace:** a *domain-general oracle framework* for live web/API/network/cloud engagements — an independent, deterministic, re-runnable confirmer that a finding is real (and, after remediation, gone). Fuse the three partial approaches: XBOW-style deterministic validators + Strix-style must-fire PoC + AIxCC-style re-run-to-verify-fix. Crucially, **the LLM (Claude) proposes; the oracle disposes** — the intelligence is never trusted for the verdict, which structurally sidesteps the 35–90% LLM-as-judge failure that plagues PyRIT/Garak and the \"trust-the-LLM\" open agents.\n\n2. **Cryptographically-provable evidence / provenance — the biggest open gap.** Nobody signs findings. **Whitespace:** every finding emitted as a **signed, tamper-evident evidence certificate** — hash-chained event log of exactly what the agent did, a reproducible PoC bundle, and an attestation binding the finding to the specific code/agent/model/toolchain that produced it (TEE/enclave-attested or in-toto/SLSA-style, up to ZK provenance). This directly answers the field's trust crisis (curl's 20% AI-slop, bounty-program DDoS, HackerOne noise): findings become **non-repudiable, independently re-verifiable, and audit/court-grade** rather than \"trust our AI.\"\n\n3. **Hard-governed scope enforcement (not prompt-level).** Everyone enforces scope via prompts + human review — which prompt-injection or a confused agent can breach. **Whitespace:** scope as a **cryptographically-bound capability** — a signed Rules-of-Engagement enforced by the *sandbox/egress/syscall* layer, not the model's discretion. Target allowlists, temporal windows (second-precision, per OWASP APTS), rate limits, and destructive-action bans enforced *deterministically*, with a tamper-evident kill switch. **Manipulation-resistant by construction:** the LLM never holds authority to expand scope because the enforcement layer, not the LLM, holds it.\n\n4. **Sovereign / air-gapped by default.** The closed cloud leaders literally cannot serve regulated, defense, national-security, or data-residency-bound customers (FedRAMP/FISMA/NIST/NATO). Only the weaker open tools can go air-gapped. **Whitespace:** the full validation + provenance + hard-governance stack running **fully self-hosted / air-gapped**, with signed-package updates and telemetry to the customer's own SIEM — a market the cloud incumbents are structurally locked out of.\n\n5. **Claude-powered reasoning + the validation moat, across the full lifecycle.** Pair frontier reasoning (Claude) as the exploration/hypothesis engine — where model quality *does* matter (Cybench/CTF capability curves show frontier models now dominate) — with the non-LLM oracle so reasoning is never trusted blindly. Then **fuse the entire lifecycle** that today is split across silos: **discover → prove (oracle) → patch → re-verify patch (oracle) → sign evidence → report.** AIxCC does discover+patch+verify but only for OSS memory bugs, no crypto, no live scope; commercial tools do discover+prove but weak patch/verify and no crypto; AI-red-team tools only probe models. **Nobody fuses AIxCC-grade proof discipline + live-target breadth + patch/fix re-verification + cryptographic provenance + hard governance + sovereignty.**\n\n**The 1-of-1 target, stated crisply:** *Take the PoV-as-oracle rigor that won DARPA AIxCC, generalize it from sandboxed OSS bugs to live authorized engagements, wrap every finding in a signed, reproducible evidence certificate, enforce scope in the network/syscall layer instead of the prompt, run it fully sovereign/air-gapped, and use Claude for reasoning while never trusting Claude for the verdict.* Each competitor owns one or two of these; the fusion of all five is unclaimed ground — and it maps cleanly onto an architecture of **deterministic oracles + evidence certificates + a hash-chained event spine + a hard safety/governance stack.**\n\n---\n\n### Primary sources to pull next (for the design doc)\n- AIxCC results & SoK: [darpa.mil/news/2025/aixcc-results](https://www.darpa.mil/news/2025/aixcc-results) · [arxiv.org/abs/2602.07666](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07666)\n- Atlantis paper (PoV-as-oracle, ensemble): [arxiv.org/abs/2509.14589](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14589) · code [github.com/Team-Atlanta/aixcc-afc-atlantis](https://github.com/Team-Atlanta/aixcc-afc-atlantis)\n- Buttercup (open-source hybrid CRS): [trailofbits.com/buttercup](https://trailofbits.com/buttercup/) · [blog.trailofbits.com/2025/08/09/...](https://blog.trailofbits.com/2025/08/09/trail-of-bits-buttercup-wins-2nd-place-in-aixcc-challenge/)\n- Big Sleep / Naptime: [projectzero.google/2024/10/from-naptime-to-big-sleep.html](https://projectzero.google/2024/10/from-naptime-to-big-sleep.html) · [projectzero.google/2024/06/project-naptime.html](https://projectzero.google/2024/06/project-naptime.html)\n- XBOW how-it-works & 1,060 attacks: [xbow.com/blog/top-1-how-xbow-did-it](https://xbow.com/blog/top-1-how-xbow-did-it) · [xbow.com/blog/we-ran-1060-autonomous-attacks](https://xbow.com/blog/we-ran-1060-autonomous-attacks)\n- Strix: [github.com/usestrix/strix](https://github.com/usestrix/strix)\n- OWASP APTS (governance standard): [owasp.org/APTS](https://owasp.org/APTS/standard/1_Scope_Enforcement/)\n- LLM-as-judge failure: [arxiv.org/pdf/2507.08794](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.08794)\n\nNote: I did this as read-only web research and did not create any plan or report files — the findings above are the deliverable. WebFetch was blocked for external domains in this environment, so all sourcing is via search-engine result extraction; the load-bearing numbers (AIxCC 18 real bugs / 43 patches, XBOW 1,060 subs / 85×, Buttercup ~90%, Cybench figures) were each corroborated across at least two independent results, and I've flagged the few forward-dated model claims as source-attributed rather than confirmed."}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":6212,"cache_read_input_tokens":51886,"output_tokens":20689,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":6212},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":20689,"cache_read_input_tokens":51886,"cache_creation_input_tokens":6212,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":6212,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdCTPqeKiXq3yPwZUR3Sk","attributionAgent":"Explore","type":"assistant","uuid":"962cfabd-ffb1-4b3a-93b8-d2b461c766b6","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:43:42.456Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
